7.1.4  Audit Objectives  
The Audit of IFMS was conducted to ascertain whether:  
• Requirement for implementation of IFMS has been put forward in complete 
form for each of its module with stakeholder consultation. 
• Implementation and effectiveness of the IFMS in the erstwhile State/ UT of 
Jammu and Kashmir enhanced financial management and decision  making 
efficiency. 
• Development of IFMS has been carried out in accordance with the work order 
and Master Service Agreement (MSA). 
• Adequate internal controls are in place during the implementation of IFMS. 
• Implementation of IFMS has been able to secure assets, ensuring data integrity 
and provided for secure and uninterrupted operations, and to check whether 
process of the implementation from the beginning has been documented 
comprehensively. 
7.1.5  Audit Criteria 
The criteria for audit were derived from the following:  
• J&K Treasury Codes 2021; 
• JK Financial Codes Volume -1 and J&K Budget Manual; 
• The Memoranda of Understanding between Finance Department, GoJ&K, 
NIC, New Delhi and National Informatics Centre Services Incorporated 
(NISCI); 
• Project Proposal of IFMS submitted (April 2010) by NIC and approved 
(October 2011) by J&K Cabinet; 
• Mission Mode Project-Computerisation of Treasuries guidelines (July 2010); 
• Detailed Project Report of IFMS; 
• Guidelines and Notifications by  Ministry of Electronics and Information 
Technology (MeitY), GoI and 
• System Design and Development documents provided by NIC. 
7.1.6  Audit Scope and Methodology 
The Audit covered the period since inception of the project in 2011-12 to 2022-23. As 
the integration of modules of IFMS, which were running independently initially, was 
done in April 2020, the database for the years 2021 -22 and 2022-23 were analysed in 
audit. Audit was conducted during May 2023 to March 2024  and examination of 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
86 
 
modules developed out of the proposed 12 modules as of March 2023 were selected for 
detailed check.  
The audit methodology involved analysis of:  
• Documents relating to project implementation. 
• Analysis of the Application Control and User Interface.  
• The Memorandum of Understanding and Service Level Agreements.  
• Analysis of databases using data dump provided.  
• Network performance and security controls with the growing number of users 
and related change in the hardware/software. 
• Availability of Information Technology (IT) infrastructure. 
An entry conference was held on 12 May 2023 with the Director General, Accounts  
and Treasuries (DGAT), Finance Department, GoJ&K in which the audit objectives, 
methodology, scope and criteria were explained. An Exit Conference was held on   
15 October 2025 with Principal Secretary Finance Department, GoJ&K during which, 
the recommendations made by audit were accepted by the Department. T he replies 
given to the audit observations have been suitably incorporated in the report. 
7.1.7  Acknowledgement  
Audit acknowledges the cooperation and assistance extended by the office of Director 
General, Accounts & Treasuries (DGAT) and staff of NIC  - J&K in conducting the 
Audit.  
7.2  Planning and Financial Management 
7.2.1  Project Planning  
The Project Planning for implementation of IFMS in J&K started with the  Finance 
Department (FD), GoJ&K directing (March 2010) the erstwhile State unit of NIC i.e. 
NIC-J&K to submit a project proposal for implement ing the  project. The FD also 
deputed a team of officers of its department as well as of  NIC to Pune to acquaint 
themselves with the IFMS model adopted by the Karnataka  and Maharashtra 
Governments. 
7.2.1.1 Preparation of Project Proposal : Implementation of IFMS without  
proposing actual and comprehensive requirements 
As per the decision taken  (February 2010) during meeting of Finance Department, 
GoJ&K4, DGAT had to submit requirements to NIC for software development, within 
one month, after going through the Karnataka IFMS model. Further, NIC was to prepare 
 
4  Under the Chairmanship of Commissioner Secretary. 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
87 
 
the Project Report within a period of one and half month s, so that further action could 
be taken regarding projection of requirement of funds to GoI/ GoJ&K.   
However, DGAT, J&K failed to submit any requirements of IFMS to NIC-J&K. In 
absence thereof, the actual and comprehensive IFMS requirements of J&K containing 
the vital information such as tasks to be performed, budget and costing, additional 
services to be rendered, terms and conditions of the contract, penalties in case of 
violations etc. could not be proposed for implementation.  
Subsequently, NIC-J&K submitted (April 2010) a project proposal based on 
Maharashtra IFMS model for implementation of IFMS in J&K. Based on the proposal 
of NIC, FD, GoJ&K formulated the DPR for ` 25.50 crore which was approved by 
J&K Cabinet in October 2011.  
Thus, DGAT relied entirely on NIC -J&K to implement IFMS in J&K potentially 
leading to inefficiencies and sub -optimal outcomes as discussed in succeeding 
Paragraphs of the report. No minutes of Steering Committee were provided to audit, in 
the absence of which audit could not ascertain if the overall supervision of the project 
was done properly. 
Non-projection of overall requirement of J&K regarding IFMS was also evident from 
inclusion of unrelated URL 5  of Arthvahini modules of Maharashtra in Software 
Requirement Specification (SRS) of GRAS, which had no functional utility in J&K. 
On being pointed out, Department stated (November 2024) that prior to implementation 
of project, comments were solicited from Planning and Development Department and 
IT Department for clearance. Decisions regarding implementation of IFMS and its 
customisation and design were made through discussion and meeting involving Finance 
Department, DGAT, NIC and Principal Accountant General.  It was further stated that 
proposal submitted by NIC was thoroughly discussed in multiple meetings of Finance 
Department and the concerned stakeholders. 
However, no such documentation or minutes of these meetings were provided to Audit. 
Further, shifting the entire responsibility of formalising the requirements of the 
erstwhile State and implementation of IFMS onto NIC does not absolve the DGAT, 
J&K of its responsibility to submit the actual and comprehensive project requirements.  
7.2.1.2 Non-formation of the Core Consultant Group (CCG) for implementation 
of IFMS 
As per DPR, experts from the all stakeholders  were expected to guide NIC for 
preparation of S ystems Requirement Specification and Business Process 
Re-engineering related efforts  to ensure that the user interface fulfils the usability 
requirements. A Core Consultants Group (CCG) should have been formed by the user 
 
5  Resolution (No. Seva Pra.  2008/ Pra kra 79/1/kosha (pra -3) dated 28 May 2008 of Government  
of Maharashtra for URL of Arthvahini (Maharas htra Module) module as  
https://arthvahini.J&K.gov.in/EChallan/frmBank/Response.php  

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
88 
 
department and should have remained active until the completion of the IFMS Project. 
CCG was mandated to have included the experts from all groups of stakeholders such 
as Accountant General ’s office , R eserve Bank of India, ITD and other financial 
institutions conducting Government business. NIC would complete the system design 
and development as per stipulated timelines and would make the system available for 
Acceptance Testing to the CCG and other team specifically designated by Finance 
department for this purpose.  
Audit noted that GoJ&K had not formed CCG as of March 2023 for implementation of 
IFMS. In absence of CCG, the audit could not ascertain whether expert 
recommendations and user requirements, for the  design and development, were taken 
into consideration and were adequately tested before being deployed. This raises doubt 
regarding project’s effective implementation. 
Department stated in its reply (November 2024) and Exit Conference (October 2025)  
that various committees were constituted over the time for smooth implementation of 
project such as State Level Committee (SLC)  and Treasury Core Group (TCG ), 
Purchase Committee, Committee for purchase of Hardware , Application Certification 
Team and working Group. It was informed in the Exit Conference that department will 
constitute a Project Steering Committee with the representatives from DGAT, NIC, 
IT Department and AG Office for regular review and functional alignment. 
These committees, however, did not include any provision for representation of said 
stakeholders and only included members of DGAT and NIC.  With respect to the 
Application Certification Team, no User Acceptance Testing ( UAT) was conducted 
except for TreasuryNet in 2011 (further discussed in Paragraph 7.5.1.1). No CCG was 
constituted as per the requirements of project proposal and the minutes of meetings of 
these committees were not made available to audit.

The issues related to the funding are further discussed in the subsequent paragraphs: 
7.2.2.1 Non-fulfilment of required conditions: L oss of central assistance of  
` 11.88 crore 
As per MMP, the implementation of project was to span two years after which NIC was 
to do handholding through Annual Maintenance Contract for another three years. The 
expenditure of ` 8.56 crore of AMC was to be met by GoJ&K. In October 2011, the 
J&K cabinet approved the DPR of IFMS by NIC-J&K. 
As per the MMP –Treasury computerisation scheme, the Central share was to be 
released to the designated State/Union Territory (UT) in three instalments of  
40 per cent, 30 per cent and 30 per cent respectively.  
The States/UTs were required to complete their project in three years and any residual 
components were allowed to be spilled over into the fourth year. However, the release 
of second and third instalments was contingent upon achieving stipulated milestones as 
outlined in Table 7.2.2.

During Exit Conference (October 2025), the Department stated that main reason for 
delay for not being able to achieve the milestones was inability to provide the internet 
connectivity to the treasuries located in the far-flung areas. 
The reply is not tenable as, not releasing of further central share was due to non-revision 
of DPR as per GoI’s instructions and non -achievement of prescribed milestones of 
MMP-Treasury computerisation guidelines, the erstwhile State/ UT had lost the central 
assistance of ` 11.88 crore for implementation of IFMS. 
7.2.3  Project Execution  
A Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) was signed (17 May 2012) between the 
Finance Department, GoJ&K, NIC , J&K and NICSI for implementation of IFMS in 
J&K. According to Clause IV of MoU, the date of start of the project was date of 
transfer of funds to NICSI (04 June 2010) or signing of MoU (17 May 2012), whichever 
was later. The MoU was valid for a period of five years from the date of start of the 
project. The MoU was to  be considered for further extension based on the mutual 
consent of all co-signatories of the MoU. Audit observed the following deficiencies in 
the project execution: 
7.2.3.1 Non-Execution of Service Level Agreement with System Integrator  
(NIC-J&K) 
As per Guidelines for Technical and Financial Support for Establishment of State Data 
Centre issued by Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (Mei tY), GoI, 
Service Level Agreement (SLA) is a document which clearly defines the levels of 
service which shall be provided by the implementing agency to its users.  
The standard level of service that would be rendered by the implementing agency within 
the framework of security includes performance criteria, availability of services, action 
to be taken in cases of a service failure and response and repair time. 
Audit observed the following in this regard: 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
91 
 
• DGAT had not entered into SLA for service availability and its monitoring with 
the implementing agency (NIC-J&K).  
• Further, the MoU signed (17 May 2012) between FD, GoJ&K, NIC and NICSI 
was also silent about SLA and the levels of service that shall be provided by NIC-J&K 
to its users. 
In absence of SLA, NIC -J&K cannot be held responsible by the user department for 
failure to implement, repair or delay of any of the services provided by them. 
Negligence of the Finance Department, GoJ&K (DGAT) to not execute SLA with the 
implementing agency, deliverables and timelines for the implementation of the IFMS 
system and it’s further maintenance were not fixed.  
Finance Department, GoJ&K in the Exit Conference accepted the Audit observation 
and assured (October 2025) that adequate steps would be taken to the ensure the 
execution of SLA going forward. 
7.2.3.2 Business Process Re-engineering exercise not undertaken 
 
Paragraph 4 of Mission Mode Project -computerisation of Treasuries  guidelines of  
July 2010 prescribes some indicative minimum deliverables such as Business Process  
Re-engineering (BPR): Re-engineering of existing processes and introduction of new 
processes where necessary to ensure synergy of processes with electronic systems.  
Audit examination disclosed the following: 
• The Finance Department, GoJ&K had not prepared an action plan for BPR and 
the failure to do so despite being a condition for release of central grants, as detailed in 
Paragraph 7.2.2.1 indicates deficient project implementation. 
• Instead of releasing of funds for BPR to NICSI in the initial phase of project 
implementation, the ` 30 lakh  were released after a delay of over 12 years in  
March 2023. However, as of June 2024, no action was taken to complete the same by 
NIC. 
In reply, DGAT accepted (June 2024) that BPR has not been undertaken yet and that in 
new module/ version, due care would be taken regarding BPR.  Further, Department 
stated (November 2024) that  BPR is a continuous process and for this purpose , an 
amount of ` 30.00 lakh were released to NICSI in March 2023. 
Reply was not acceptable  for the fact that t he Finance Department had neglected the 
BPR process and developed the IFMS applications on adhoc basis without taking into 
consideration requirements of the erstwhile State/ UT and making corresponding 
changes in the codes and manuals to cater to the needs of digital systems.  Audit 
observation noted in this regard were discussed in Paragraph 7.3.3.2, 7.3.4.1, 7.3.4.3, 
7.3.4.5 and 7.3.5.1.

7.2.3.3 Diversion of funds of upgradation of State Data Centre 
As per the Paragraph 1.6.4 of DPR, strategy to integrate with other State Services viz. 
With State Data Centre - IFMS Central Server is to be located at NIC Data Centre 
Jammu, and once the State Data Centre becomes operational, the IFMS applications 
will be migrated/ switched over to State Data Centre. 
The estimated costs proposed (April 2010) by NIC-J&K for establishing IFMS Central 
Server at SDC was ` 39 lakh. However, the cost of Server Infrastructure was  revised 
(January 2011) to ` 3.26 crore in DPR prepared by FD, GoJ&K. The GoJ&K released  
` 5.26 crore (up-to March 2023) to NICSI for upgradation of SDC for hosting/ storage 
of IFMS applications. 
Audit noted the following deficiencies in this regard: 
• Instead of carrying out upgradation of SDC for hosting/storage of IFMS 
applications, NIC irregularly hosted all the applications of IFMS on NIC Mini Data 
Centre at Jammu, as detailed in Table 7.2.3, which was originally meant to be used as 
a backup site. 
Table 7.2.3: Details of hosting of application in NIC Mini Data Centres 
(Source: Information furnished by DGAT) 
• Despite the release of ` 5.26 crore (up to March 2023) by DGAT, progress of 
work done on upgradation of SDC for hosting/storage of IFMS applications was not 
available with DGAT as of April 2024. 
• As of April 2024, NIC Mini Data Centre was being used as main IFMS central 
data centre without a supporting backup site. This poses a potential threat to the security 
and integrity of sensitive data such as budget figures, personnel information, and 
payrolls etc. of the IFMS project.  
• Further, t he continued possession of IFMS data by NIC instead of by 
DGAT/Finance Department, GoJ&K, has the potential to thwart regular operations in 
case of an adverse event.

upgradation of Data Centre and strengthening the IT infrastructure under IFMS and  
have been utilised for recurring operational needs. It was stated in the reply that no

7.3  Project Implementation

computerisation of treasuries is crucial for achieving greater efficiencies, reducing cost, 
eliminating redundancies and adopting modern public expenditure practices. The 
guidelines (July 2010) for Treasury Computerisation, a Mission Mode Project (MMP) 
under National e -Governance Plan (NeGP), envisioned minimum set of deliverables 
(Table 7.3.1) as outcomes of treasury computerisation/IFMS.

to, even after the period of 11 years and 05 months11 as of March 2023.  
Thus, despite lapse of over a decade since the inception of the IFMS project, many of 
its intended objectives and outcomes remain unachieved, due to the non/  partial 
implementation of key modules as of March 2023.

the MoU i.e. 17 May 2012.

applications of IFMS ranging between 42 months and 127 months against the scheduled 
timeline fixed in the DPR, as detailed in Table 7.3.2.

Though the TreasuryNet application was rolled out on 01 April 2013 but it was initially

Reply was not acceptable as already discussed in Paragraph 7.3.1 many of the modules

fully implementing the IFMS in the UT has impacted on the objectives of integrating 
erstwhile State/UT finances like real time access of state finances, processing of bills

7.3.3  Implementation of Budget Module (BEAMS application) 
As p er the  DPR approved (January 2011) by Ministry of Finance, GoI for 
implementation of IFMS, the BEAMS application was to be used for: 
• Estimation / distribution of budget and authori sation of expenditure in online 
mode. 
• Reappropriation of funds within prescribed limits and withdrawal/  surrender of 
budget grants. 
• Real-time monitoring of expenditure.

The workflow of Budget Module in BEAMS application is given in Chart 7.3.1. 
Chart 7.3.1: Workflow of Budget Module in BEAMS application 
 
Audit observations regarding the development and implementation of BEAMS 
application are detailed in Paragraph 7.3.3.1 to 7.3.3.3. 
7.3.3.1 Internal Control of B EAMS Application- Absence of Client -side and 
Server-side validation 
Audit examination of the front-end of the BEAMS application  identified a few issues 
even after 10 years of implementation in the budget allocation process within the login 
of Finance Department as detailed below: 
• The system allow ed the budget sanctioning authority to input a quarterly 
percentage of budget allocation exceeding 100 per cent. Although Audit did not observe 
any such mistake in the database but t his loophole might lead to the insertion of 
erroneous data into the database. 
• The absence of both client-side and server-side validation to not being able to 
validate the above discussed entry means errors may not get flagged until they result in 
erroneous calculations or grant of demand.  
• Users receive no warning if they mistakenly enter invalid data, compromising 
the system’s ability to minimise human error and no warning is given to user in case an 
error is committed while saving data in the database.  
While accepting the audit observation, Finance Department stated (June / 
November 2024) that at the initial stage there was possibility of release of more than 


Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
97 
 
100 per cent funds but now the system has been improved and there is no such chance 
as budget figures under a scheme or detailed head are freezed at all the levels. 
7.3.3.2 Non-implementation of functionalities of BEAMS 
Audit noted following deficiencies due to non-implementation/ partial implementation 
of the functionalities in BEAMS: 
a) Budget Estimates once prepared are submitted to the Administrative Departments 
and later to Finance Department (FD) for finali sation. However, the scrutiny of 
discussion sheets revealed that justification for sharp increase or decrease in the 
final budget figures are not being recorded in the discus sion sheets generated on 
BEAMS. 
Chart 7.3.2: Procedure for submission of Budget Estimates 
Test check of the discussion sheet s reveals that budget estimates  of demands for the 
financial year 2022 -23 were reworked by the UT Finance Department in a range of  
(-) 80 per cent to 43 per cent. 
General Administrative Department (Demand– 01), the Budget Estimates for Object 
Head - 037 (Professional and Special Service Charges) under Sub -Head  
1825– Planning Department were submitted ‘Zero’ by the Department. However, the 
Finance Department GoJK reworked the Budget Estimates to ` 63.00 lakh. 
Thus, the budget estimates were increased/decreased by Finance Department without 
any justification of any such decrease and increase in the initial budget estimates 
submitted by DDOs. 
In reply, DGAT/ Department accepted the audit observation and stated (March 2025) 
that option for recording the justification/ remarks would be incorporated for the budget 
estimates for the coming financial years. 
b) Immediately after the budget is passed by the Parliament/ Legislative Assembly 
with the approval of Appropriation Act, the allocations are updated for the next financial 
year.

Chart 7.3.3: Procedure for Allocation of Budget 
Audit noticed that, t o achieve this, the next financial year is manually initiated  in the 
financial year table by executing an update command directly in to the database by 
Project Management Unit (PMU cell)  which should have automatically been done by 
the IFMS system. 
Non-implementation of the business-rules in the BEAMS module does not facilitate the 
allocation of funds for the next financial year through a dedicated user interface for the 
Finance Department and running direct operations on the database by the PMU Cell 
creates a risk for data integrity and security.   
During Exit Conference Department (October 2025) accepted the audit observation and 
stated that NIC has been asked to implement mandatory validation fields and version 
control for budget updates. 
c) As per the records produced, funds amounting to ` 220.98 crore for the financial 
year 2023-24 and ` 624.77 crore for 2024-25 were reappropriated. 
Chart 7.3.4: Procedure for Reappropriation of Funds 
Audit noticed that the reappropriation of funds was being processed through physical 
files instead of through BEAMS and amounts of reappropriations were being manually 
updated in the system at the backend and directly into the database. 
A test check of the manual reappropriation orders revealed that out of total funds of 
` 624.77 crore reappropriated for the FY 2024-25, an amount of ` 515.72 crore found 
to be approved after the close of the financial year i.e. during the year 2025-26 
Thus, due to non-implementation of reappropriation of funds through frontend system 
in BEAMS and non -availability of any tracking or approval process in the IFMS for  
reappropriation of funds, the system could not ensure if these funds were made available 
in time to facilitate intended expenditures, and the expenditure was incurred after due 
approval of reappropriation of funds. 
No specific reply has been given by the Department on this issue.

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
99 
 
d) Audit noticed that the surrender functionality for the funds allocated has not 
been developed in the BEAMS application. Further, it has been observed that surrender 
statement was prepared at DDO level manually, but no surrender statement was 
prepared at the level of Finance Department until May 2025  and was not being 
submitted for accounting purposes. 
In reply, DGAT accepted the audit observation and stated (March 2025) that surrenders 
are not processed through IFMS and Budget Estimated (BE)/ Revised Estimates (RE) 
of a particular year, figures adopted under BE minus the figures adopted under RE are 
considered as surrender. 
The reply is not acceptable as treating the difference between BE and RE as surrender 
is a notional adjustment, not an accounting process. As per prescribed procedure, 
surrenders should be compiled from DDO -level statements, consolidated by the 
Finance Department, and recorded in account s. The absence of any such statements 
since the formation of the UT reflects both a control deficiency in BEAMS (absence of 
surrender functionality) and a systemic lapse in capturing actual savings, affecting 
budget discipline and financial accuracy.  
Thus, due to non-implementation of the surrender facility in BEAMS, the objective of 
real-time accounting of surrenders and reallocations has not been achieved, and manual 
compilation continues without reliable surrender figures.

7.3.4  Implementation of Bill Preparation Module (JKPaySys application) 
NIC-J&K developed JKPaySys application for State/ UT for implementation of Bill 
Preparation module (BPM) of IFMS. JKPaySys is used to prepare various types of bills,

through Maker and C hecker 13, for sending them to treasuries for reali sation. The 
workflow of BPM is given in Chart 7.3.5.

Audit observations regarding the development and implementation of JKPaySys 
application are detailed in Paragraph 7.3.4.1 to 7.3.4.5. 
7.3.4.1 Internal Control of JKPaySys Application 
(I) Improper implementation of Suspension functionality in JKPaySys 
Data analysis of the dataset for the financial year 2021-22 and 2022-23 containing 1,688 
and 2,470 records respectively relating to suspension and revocation orders highlights 
the following anomalies and inconsistencies: 
• Suspension order numbers are missing in 1,064 records (63 per cent) and 1,133 
records (46 per cent) during the financial year 2021-22 and 2022-23 respectively. 
During 2021-22, DDOs - Block Mawar, Deputy Inspecter General of Police Rajouri 
& Poonch and Regional Director Social Forestry suspended employees  for which 
audit noted these suspensions have unreasonable order dates i.e. 12  June 1935,  
06 April 1920, 15 May 1900 respectively reflecting poor data validation. 
• Out of 1,162 and 1,785 reinstated employees during the financial year 2021-22 
and 2072-23, revocation order numbers were missing in 339 records (29 per cent) and 
437 records (24 per cent) respectively.  
 
 
13  Maker is responsible for initiating or drafting a transaction while the Checker reviews the Maker’s submission 
ensuring compliance, accuracy, completeness and availability of budget. 


Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
102 
 
During 2021-22, DDOs - Accounts Officer Office of Divisional Commissioner, Joint 
Director Fisheries Projects Kashmir Division, Public Health Engineering Department 
filled the future revocation and revocation order date i.e. 10  June 2050,  
01 March 2030, 01  March 2029 for the employees suspended in 14  June 2019,  
01 June 2014, 20 September 2021 respectively without restoring i.e. restoration flag 
in the system was set to Zero. 
• Suspension of employees was revoked before the actual suspension date in  
08 cases during 2021-22 and in 17 cases during 2022-23. 
Above shows that the functionality of suspension is implemented without analysing the 
business process and not placing enough control in the system. Further, the business 
rule for the suspension facility is not implemented properly as the suspension of 
employees does not deactivate suspended employee in the system and the same is being 
carried out manually through “Employee Info Page”. 
Further, Audit scrutiny of the “Suspension Page” of JKPaySys application revealed that 
there was an absence of client-side or server-side validation as illustrated below: 
• There are four14 different functional dates on the web page, if user errs in filling 
the date, the user is unable to delete the same in the frontend once chosen. The facility 
for deletion/ modification of “these Dates” could only be done by support team of NIC 
from the backend. 
• Further, if a user selects “No” from the option provided under  the option  
“Whether Restored ” the related fields such as “Order N umber of Restoration 15 ”, 
“Order Date of Restoration ” and “Date of Restoration ” are not disabled. These fields  
should ideally get disabled so that no data is stored for these fields  in the database .  
However, audit noted entries in these fields. Also, “date of restoration” field in the table 
was automatically getting filled with a default date of 01 January 1900 even in case of 
a null value.  
This indicated absence of the following conditional input controls: 
a) Control Weakness:  The system fails to disable or block dependent fields 
(“Order Number of Restoration,” etc.) when “Whether Restored” is marked as “No.” 
This indicates missing validation and business rule enforcement. 
b) Data Integrity Issue:  The field auto -filling with “01 January 1900” reflects poor 
null handling at the database level, leading to misleading records. 
c) Real-World Risk:  Such entries can distort restoration status in MIS reports and 
audit trails.

During Exit Conference , Department accepted the audit observation and stated 
(October 2025) that necessary corrective measures for integration and up-dation of data 
shall be taken-up in co-ordination with all the stakeholders. 
(II) Absence of input validation in respect of e mployees getting service 
 extension  
As per Department of Personnel and Training (DoPT), Ministry of Finance (MoF), GoI 
Circular (01 December 2022) on reti rement and extension of service , every 
Government servant shall retire from service on the afternoon of the last day of the 
month in w hich he attains the age of 60 years  and n o government servant shall be 
granted extension in service beyond the age of retirement of 60 years except in special 
conditions. 
The JKPaySys application provides the facility of extension of service to retired 
employees through “Service Extension” module. Data analysis of the data set obtained 
for the financial year 2021 -22 and 2022 -23 having 153 and 374 records respectively 
revealed that necessary fields like duration of extension, expiry of extension and 
superannuation date etc ., were not recoded in the database. In the available database 
following abnormalities and inconsistencies were noted by audit: 
 
 


Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
104 
 
• In 11 cases and 26 cases during financial year 2021-22 and 2022-23 respectively 
the date of recording the extension was found to be later than the date of extension. 
• In 70 cases and 183 cases of 2021 -22 and 2022-23 respectively, extension was 
granted in far advance of date of extension, ranging from 365 days to 10,658 days.  
• During 2022-23 in 5 active cases the duplication of the extension was noted 
where both the extension was active. 
Above issues show that the functionality of service extension is implemented without 
analysing the business process and not placing enough controls in the system. 
Further, Audit scrutiny ( March 2024 ) of “ Service Extension page ” of JKPaySys 
application revealed that instead of showing only superannuated employees in the 
“Service Extension page”, the page  was listing both serving and superannuated 
employees under Active and Inactive tab respectively.  
The system should not allow any serving employee16 to be listed for service extension 
even before superannuation. Audit, upon testing, noted that if a serving employee 
was selected for extension i.e. before the date of superannuation, the system readily 
accepts the values and stores them. 
Thus, there was no internal validation both on the client side and server side to restrict 
the service extension feature only to superannuated employees . Absence of such 
internal controls could lead to entry of misleading and wrong data in the database.  
While accepting the audit observation, the  Department stated (November 2024) that 
validation of the record for employees retiring in next three years shall be done shortly. 
(III)  Non-Functional Utilisation Certificates facility in JKPaySys 
Audit noticed that although a link for submission of Utilisation Certificates (UCs) was 
included in the JKPaySys application, the user interface does not facili tate (as of  
March 2024) any functionality to the DDOs to upload the Utilisation Certificates. 
Analysis of data reveal ed that out of 6 ,728 DDOs , only 26 and 08 Utilisation 
Certificates were uploaded by 12 and 07 DDOs during 2021 -22 and 2022 -23 
respectively (though the functionality of uploading UCs was not found functional 
during audit).  And, in respect of these 34 submitted UCs, the amount of UCs was not 
recorded in the database and 14 UCs did not accompany any supporting documents.  
The above  issues highlight  the improper implementation of business rule for the 
submission of Utilisation Certificates where amount, due date for submission and actual 
date of UC submission were not being recorded while the feature of entering the details 
of the UCs was functional in JKPaySys . Further system fail ed to stop the submission 
 
16  (CPIS ID: KXXXXXXXXXX4, Date of birth: 22  February 1993 and Date of superannuation:  
28 February 2053). 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
105 
 
of UC without uploading necessary documentation which indicates the weak validation 
control in place. 
In reply, DGAT/ Department stated (June 2024/ November 2024) that the facility was 
provided for uploading the UC/ Red Account in respect of Grant in Aid received by 
Departments/ Bodies. It further stated that although facility was operational, no UC was 
uploaded by these departments/ bodies. 
The reply is not acceptable, as the UC upload functionality was not operational  as of 
March 2024, indicating a lapse in enforcing necessary application controls in IFMS to 
ensure proper accounting and monitoring of expenditure. 
During Exit Conference , the  Department stated (October 2025)  that UC submission 
facility is being upgraded and will be made available in the next phase. 
7.3.4.2 Discrepancies in Database of JKPaySys Application 
The JKPaySys application uses data from several master tables such as  “DDO Master 
Table”, “DDO Office Details”, “Departmental Master Table” etc.  
Audit scrutiny of data of these master tables brought out the deficiencies detailed in 
Table 7.3.6. 
Table 7.3.6: Details of deficiencies in the master tables of the database 
Sl. No. Name of the database table field No of Records 
1 Mobile number was either blank or was having null value 32 
2 Email ID was blank 23 
3 TAN number was either blank or was having null value 46 
4 PAN number was either blank or was having null value 13 
5 GST number was either blank or having null value 841 
6 Account number was either blank or having null value 50 
(Source: JKPaySys Data) 
In the absence of proper format and validation checks, the system allowed storage of 
invalid or incomplete data in key fields such as PAN, account number, and contact 
details. This resulted in the presence of erroneous (“garbage”) values in the database. 
Such weak data validation controls carry the risk of affecting multifactor authentication 
or user identification if mobile numbers or emails are incorrect, and may also impair 
the functioning of dependent modules related to tax and accounting as further discussed 
in Paragraph 7.3.4.5. 
In reply, DGAT/ Department stated (June 2024/ November 2024) that the GST, TAN 
numbers, email IDs and mobile numbers of DDOs are not mandatory at present, but 
they have been kept for any future requirement. It was  further stated  that after 
integration with the GST portal (started in 2024), the DDO cannot generate bill without 
a GST number.  With regards blank account numbers of DDO , it was stated 
(November 2024) that these particular DDOs were not operating in the treasury as no 
payment was made by these DDOs and they were created solely for treasury receipts.

However, no documentation was provided to audit to substantiate the claim regarding 
such DDOs or to confirm that these data fields were genuinely not required. 
7.3.4.3 Non-implementation of functionalities of JKPaySys 
a) Non implementation of e -Sanctions: As per provisions 12.4.0 of the Jammu 
and Kashmir Budget manual, sanction of the competent authority to the incurring of 
expenditure remains valid until the funds necessary to make the sanction operative have 
not been refused.  
It was observed that, Executive engineer/  Deputy Director Mechanical & Works. 
(M&W), has power to grant contracts for individual works up to ` 40 lakh and the 
works above ` 40 lakh could only be granted by the Chief Engineer. However, it was 
observed that the contract of the work valuing ` 954 lakh was awarded by the 
Deputy Director (M&W) Jammu which is beyond its financial power. 
In reply , DGAT/ Department stated (March 2025) that the sanction orders were 
generated at the time of release of funds. Further, the delegation of power can be 
checked at treasury and PaySys level manually. 
Thus, the reply confirms that the system lacks the requisite business rule and is not able 
to enforce the control of the delegation of financial powers. 
It was noted that Budget Release/Sanction order is generated from IFMS (BEAMS) 
application at time of release of budget or additional budget to DDOs or transfer of 
Funds from one DDO to another.  
Audit also noted that these releases were being considered as the final sanction for 
expenditure by the DDOs. Further, due to the non -implementation of e -sanctions 
module in the  bill preparation module (JKP aySys) of  IFMS, no such facility for 
generating the financial sanctions against each expenditure was available.  The facility 
for linking the manually generated financial sanctions containing order/number date 
with the bills in JKPaySys was not implemented.  
b) Non implementation of AC/DC bill facility:  An Abstract Contingent (AC) 
bill is used when DDO requires money in advance for meeting the expenditure of 
contingent nature , where the final classification and supporting vouchers were not 
available at the time of drawl. It requires the submission of detailed accounts within 
specified time.  
Detailed Contingent (DC) bills are prepared in support of a charge already drawn on 
AC Bill and is subjected to countersigned by a Controlling Authority. However, audit 
noted that the functionality for submission of Detailed Contingent Bill for adjustment 
of Abstract Contingent Bill previously drawn was not implemented in IFMS . In the 
absence of these functionalities in the IFMS system, the system was not able to enforce 
the necessary business rules in the workings of the DDOs. 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
107 
 
During the Exit Conference Department accepted the audit observation and stated 
(October 2025) that interface for submission of Red Account (DC Bill) for AC bills 
through JKPaySys and submission of the same to Accountant General Office shall be 
developed by NIC. 
7.3.4.4 Bill generation on JKPaySys without Budget allotment through BEAMS 
The provisions of the Jammu Kashmir Budget  Manual prescribes  the control over 
expenditure by drawing and disbursing officers. It envisages that the bills submitted to 
the treasury should be properly and correctly classified according to the budget 
allocations made to them by respective Budget Controlling Officers.  
Further, the JKPaySys user manual envisages that sending of bill to Checker is enabled 
only when the required budget is available. However, data analysis of the transactions 
generated through the JKPaySys application based on the condition of non-availability 
of budget reveals the following missing controls in JKPaySys application: 
• During the financial year 2021-22, five DDOs for a combination of 47 schemes 
were able to generate bill amounting to ` 75.06 crore; and 
• During 2022 -23, six DDOs were able to generate bill amounting to  
` 83.23 crore for a combination of 51 schemes  in the absence of proper allocation of 
funds through BEAMS.  
This indicates that there was an ineffective mechanism within IFMS applications to 
exercise the control over expenditure. This defeats the overall purpose of having an 
integrated system. 
No specific reply has been furnished by the Department as of January 2026. 
7.3.4.5 Review of Agency Master under JKPaySys 
Agency Master is the master table that stores details of all agencies ( Departments, 
contractors, vendors, beneficiaries, etc.) that receive or make payments through the 
system. 
Analysis of the data obtained for the financial year of 2021-22 and 2022 -23 revealed 
the following irregularities and inconsistencies: 
• In 2021-22, a total of 24,023 agencies/  beneficiaries were found duplicated in 
the database, while in 2022 -23, the number of duplicates increased to 34,188. This 
happened due to absence of master data of beneficiary/ agencies in the database. 
On test check of the records of DDOs, the f ollowing discrepancies were noted due to 
non-validation of GST rules in the IFMS system: 
 
 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
108 
 
a) Multiple GST numbers for same GST vendor: 
The agency ‘Jammu Co -Operative Wholesale Ltd Super Bazar’ with account 
number 12030401000****2 was found repeatedly entered 458 times in the database. 
Further, the agency was linked to 40 different GST numbers and payment s of  
` 12.59 lakh and ` 11.25 lakh were made during 2021-22 and 2022-23 respectively. 
This indicates the absence of integration between GST Department and IFMS. 
b) Use of wrong GST numbers not belonging to the GST vendor: 
Agency ‘N J Traders’ with account number 94140 ****7 was found to be entered  
31 times in the database with five different GST numbers. A total payment of  
` 13.89 lakh and ` 25.11 lakh were made to agency during 2021 -22 and 2022 -23. 
Further, analysis of the GST numbers revealed that these GST numbers pertain to 
different agencies or were invalid. As GST number ‘01BLWPJ955****P’ was found 
to be of the N J Trader, whereas the GST number ‘01BHUPG234****V’ was found 
to be of RSR VENTURES and Other three GST numbers were found invalid. Thus, 
IFMS JK has failed to place any control to check these account numbers and enforce 
the rules of GST tax collection on the contractors/ agencies. 
c) Use of cancelled GST number by the non-GST compliant vendor: 
As per the extant GST Rules, a non-GST compliant vendor cannot issue tax invoices. 
GST number 01AYCPA595 ****5 associated with the vendor M/S KHAN 
COMPUTERS MOBILE SALE & SERVICE was registered on 22  December 2017 
and cancelled suo-moto from 12 February 2020. The vendor last filled the GSTR3B 
on 01  January 2020.  Analysis of the GST number reveal ed that despite having 
cancelled GST number the vendor continued to make the taxable supply  to the 
“Deputy Commissioner Poonch” .  Taxable supplies of ` 4.24 lakh during 2021-22 
and ` 40.69 lakh ( altered rates of GST ) during 2022-23 respectively was made by 
agency. Thus, non-integration of GST with IFMS has failed to restrict the trading 
activity as well as evasion of GST amounting to ` 6.90 lakh. 
• Further, audit noted that 79 bills of 18 agencies/ beneficiaries amounting to  
` 20.21 crore and 377 bills of 12 agencies/ beneficiaries amounting to ` 7.09 crore were 
paid respectively during the financial year 2021 -22 and 2022-23 for which records of 
the agencies were not found in the system. 
This reflects the lack of integration of IFMS with external systems such as GST. 
Further, allowing payments for the vendors outside the Agency Master  or multiple 
recording of same vendor with multiple GST numbers  undermines the objective of an 
integrated payment mechanism within IFMS. 
While accepting the audit observation, DGAT/ Department/NIC -JK stated  
(September 2025) that IFMS was not integrated with the GST system initially. 
Integration began in 2024. Before that, DDOs manually entered GST numbers, and no 
automated validation or duplication check existed. 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
109 
 
The reply is not acceptable as audit noted instances of the same vendor or agency listed 
multiple times with different GSTINs in the agency master table  (JKPaySys). Further, 
the system only verified if the GSTIN is valid and active. It does not fetch the details 
such as the Name/ Type of taxpayer nor does the system stop the duplication of vendors 
in the JKPaySys system’s agency master. 
7.3.5  Integration among BEAMS, JKPaySys and TreasuryNet application 
One of the main objectives of IFMS  was to 
ensure that  allocations made  under BEAMS, 
expenditure booked under JKPaySys and 
actual expenditure incurred under TreasuryNet 
were all seamlessly integrated leaving no 
transaction untraceable across these 
applications.  
After allotment of budget by GoJ&K through 
BEAMS, DDOs submit the bills to Treasury 
Officers through JKPaySys. Treasury Officers 
scrutinise the bills submitted by the DDOs and 
authorise the payment through TreasuryNet.  
Audit observations regarding integration 
among BEAMS, JKPaySys and TreasuryNet 
application are detailed in succeeding 
Paragraphs:   
 
7.3.5.1 Review of Integration of different IFMS applications 
Audit scrutiny of JKPaySys and TreasuryNet applications data for the years 2021 -22 
and 2022-23 to check the integration of the applications revealed the following issues: 
Manual processing of bills: When a bill is prepared in JKPaySys, a unique identifier 
“Bill ID/No” along with a bar code on the hard copy of bill is generated . This id is 
meant to be a unique identifier of the bill for both JKPaySys and TreasuryNet. 
• Non-recording of essential information in the IFMS system: It was observed 
that Treasury officials could not fetch bills submitted by DDOs online unless physical 
copies of the bills containing barcode and supporting documents  were received in 
Treasuries from DDOs  as per the design requirement in the Software Requirements 
Specification (SRS).  
Upon scanning the barcode from the physical copy of the bill,  only details such as 
amount of bill, DDO name, head of account to which the payment is  to be debited, 
deductions to be made from the bills etc. are fetched in the TreasuryNet interface.  
Chart 7.3.6: IFMS Integration Workflow 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
110 
 
Since the physical copies of bills are required to be submitted to treasury  to fetch the 
details of bills , the objective of submission of bills/  vouchers online and complete 
digitisation of the workflow for bill processing was not achieved as all these different 
applications were not fully integrated. Further, the circular (April 2019) of F inance 
Department17, GoJ&K envisages that with implementation of JKPaySys, only e -bills 
would be processed by Treasuries and no manual bill would be entertained with effect 
from 1 May 2019.  
• Preparation of Bill outside the IFMS system: It was observed that out of total 
11.50 lakh bills, 11,534 non -pension bills were manually processed by 317 DDOs 
during 2021-22 and out of total 12.94 lakh bills, 2,281 non-pension bills were manually 
processed by 158 DDOs in the year 2022-23.

database. This indicates that the system had allowed manual processing of the bills  in 
TreasuryNet which were not generated on JKPaySys in violation of directions of 
Finance Department. Resultantly, the amount of bills booked under TreasuryNet 
application was higher than that in JKPaySys. These instances are detailed in

and the technical inefficiency of the system in ensuring seamless data transfer.

the unauthorised alterations in the IFMS applications. The discrepancies noted during

ascertain in how many cases tampering as discussed above of bill both on physical 
voucher and on database level has taken place. Manual interventions like these makes 
the IFMS transactions unreliable.  
In reply , DGAT/ Department/NIC -JK stated (September 2025) that,  initially, 
corporations and autonomous bodies were given PAO codes as they operated  
off-treasury. Later, to ensure full treasury operations, the AG office allotted them AG 
codes, converting all PAO codes accordingly. 
The reply is not acceptable, as the issue pertains to unauthori sed changes in DDO/AG 
codes where no corporations or autonomous bodies were involved. The matter clearly 
indicates manipulation in DDO/AG codes, which cannot be justified by the cited reason 
of conversion from PAO to AG codes. 
(ii) Difference in amount s in bill generated on JKPaySys and passed on 
TreasuryNet 
During data analysis and further test check it was noted that two bills of 2022-23 were 
generated for ` 17,090 and ` 25,800 on JKPaySys, but passed for ` 17,190 and  
` 48,512 respectively. This clearly indicates the bills data is being manipulated at 
database level. In absence of database log , audit could not ascertain the reliability of 
transactions generated through IFMS applications. 
In reply, DGAT/ Department stated (June 2024/ November 2024) that due to increase 
in number of transactions during March , incorrect data was  being transferred to 
TreasuryNet application from JKPaySys. It was further stated that the  issue was  
identified and fixed. 
The stated reason of data transmission error due to heavy transaction load is not 
plausible, as modern financial applications are designed to ensure data integrity 
irrespective of transaction volume. Moreover, one of the affected bills pertains to 
January, not March, contradicting the explanation. In the absence of database logs, the 
possibility of unauthorised or uncontrolled alteration of bill data during transmission 
or at the database level cannot be ruled out, indicating inadequate integrity controls 
within the IFMS environment. 
(iii) Difference in Detailed Head on JKPaySys than on TreasuryNet:  
Audit observed that the detailed head associated with a bill in the JKPaySys application 
differed from the one recorded in the TreasuryNet application for the same transaction 
in many cases. Such discrepancies indicate a lack of data integrity between systems and 
raise concerns of unauthorised modifications in the transaction using the database.

transaction. During analysis of data base, it was noted that scheme code of nine 
transactions of 2021-22 were different in JKPaySys and TreasuryNet database.

server-to-server integration was in a nascent stage.  
The reply is not acceptable  as variations in transactional fields such as scheme codes 
cannot be attributed to integration maturity issues, as these involve alteration of core 
accounting data at the database level. Such changes in scheme codes compromise the 
integrity of transaction reco rds and may lead to misclassification of budget and 
expenditure figures at both DDO and treasury levels. This reflects inadequate validation 
and control mechanisms within the IFMS environment.

The analysis of data shows that the voucher numbers that were generated and associated 
with the bills on Treasury Net application were found to be different than the reported

7.3.6  Implementation of C&AG Module 
MMP- Treasury Computerisation guidelines issued (July 2010) under the National  
e-Governance Plan (NeGP) stipulate that computerisation of treasuries shall facilitate 
audit of government offices. For this purpose, data samples are to be provided to audit 
for verification and ensure quick availability of audit reports of C&AG along wit h 
reference data for Legislative oversight. 
Audit observed that the Finance Department, GoJ&K had not developed a dedicated 
portal for the office of AG (Audit) J&K to facilitate audit parties by on boarding them  
to an Interface where they can easily access the basic records 19  of the auditee 
department.  
 
19  Budgetary allocations, administrative approvals, technical sanctions, sanction orders, digitally signed 
vouchers/bills, cash books, geo tagging of works, current status of works, stock registers, sanction/effective 
strength etc.  

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
115 
 
In reply, Department stated (November 2024) that NIC was prepared to share data 
through Application Programming Interface (APIs) with the AG office as and when 
directed. 
The reply is not acceptable as API based access to the IFMS data is not yet provided to 
AG (Audit). Thus, despite lapse of ten years since the initiation of IFMS project, the 
audit module of IFMS project has not been designed and developed in consultation with 
the office of AG (Audit) J&K and digital infrastructure has not been created by GoJ&K 
to enable conducting audit online.   
7.4  IT Security 
Information Security relates to the protection of information assets against the risk of 
unauthorised access, operational discontinuity, misuse, unauthorised disclosure, 
modification, or damage. Information is considered as valuable asset of the organisation 
and should therefore be protected by ensuring confidentiality, integrity, and availability. 
7.4.1  Access of IFMS Applications 
7.4.1.1 Weak logical access control of IFMS Applications 
Audit noted following deficiencies in logical access control in IFMS applications: 
• To access both BEAMS and JKPaySys applications, a valid login id and a 
password is required. For the new users the log in id and password (system  
generated-one time) is shared on email. Upon logging in, the applications prompt users 
to mandatorily change the system generated password before carrying out any business 
or transaction. T here were mainly two types of users of these application s namely  
1. The Maker and 2. The Checker, whose roles 20 were defined as per the application 
manuals of GoJ&K.  
Audit observed that the  Maker and Checker both were using the same L og-in ids but 
different passwords implying that they were differentiated/ identified solely on the basis 
of the password. 
In reply, DGAT/ Department stated (June 2024/ November 2024) that user ID contains 
two fields for the passwords of Maker and Checker and these cannot be identical.  
The reply is not acceptable as it does not address the core issue of both roles operating 
under a single user ID, which violates basic access control principles and weakens user 
accountability. 
• Audit examination of master data of TreasuryNet Application revealed that 
passwords in respect of 1,010 users had never been changed as of January  2024 in 
contravention to the MeitY Guidelines that the passwords should be changed at least 
once every three months and that users shall not be able to reuse previous passwords . 
 
20  Maker drafts the budget estimation and checker approves the budget estimation. The draft can only be generated 
or modified by the Maker while the Checker can either approve or reject the draft. 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
116 
 
Without periodic password changes, users' credentials may remain unchanged for 
extended periods, making them susceptible to credential stuffing attacks where 
attackers use leaked username/password combinations to gain unauthorised access to 
accounts.   
Further, multi -factor authentication such as biometric, web/app -based authenticator, 
One Time Password etc., which is an extra layer of security that is used to establish the 
identity of a valid user, was not in place. This renders the system more vulnerab le to 
potential fraud where a person with malicious intension unethically obtains the 
credentials and commits unauthorised transactions. 
In reply, Department stated (November 2024) that password change facility for Maker 
was granted to Checker so as to assign role of Maker to any employee in the office. In 
case of Maker password, change default password was sent via mail with confirmation 
via phone call. It was further added that in next phase, password change will be made 
OTP-based to enhance security. 
However, reply was silent about non -implementation of ma ndatory periodic change 
policy. 
• Audit noted that  “forgot password” facility of BEAMS application  is 
improperly implemented as it requires the current password to create a new password.  
Further, forgot password facility entails following deficiencies:  
(i) As discussed earlier, enabling the Checker to change the password of the Maker 
and not allowing the latter to change it himself/ herself is in violation of IT 
password policy of not sharing the user’s credentials.  Further, issues related to 
using single User ID is noted in the Paragraph 7.4.1.2. 
(ii) If the NIC/ Checker changes the password of the Maker, the system does not 
provide for an audit trail to identify the person who had made such a change and 
whether any change was made or not. 
(iii) If the Checker forgets password, the system does not provide for setting a new 
password for Checker and instead the Checker has to rely on NIC support team/ 
Budget Controlling authorities for setting a new password. 
While accepting the audit observation, Department stated (November 2024) that “forgot 
password” option for maker was provided to checker to change password. It was further 
stated that “Forgot Password” facility for the Checker was available with Budget 
Controlling authorities and NIC support team had no role in it. 
The reply of the department is not acceptable for the fact that there was improper 
implementation of the Password policy for the various systems implemented. Also, the 
reply was silent about missing audit trail of the password change events. 
 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
117 
 
7.4.1.2 Access Authorisation Failure: Simultaneous access to the Maker and 
the Checker without password 
While reviewing the Checker login of the JKPaySys application, audit noted that 
clicking on a one particular link in the checker login allows the Checker to perform all 
the Maker functions. 
For instance, when an authorised Checker logs in on the home page of the JKPaySys 
under the Masters tab  and clicks the option of “Add Bulk Allowances ”, the system 
changes his/ her role to Maker for all subsequent functions in the session without using 
Maker login credentials. 
Thus, failure of the NIC-J&K to guard against simultaneous access of the Checker and 
the Maker without need for password may lead to critical risks relating to Data security, 
User privacy and system integrity.  
While accepting the audit observation, the Department stated (November 2024) that the 
issue was taken up with the NIC who acknowledged to correct it promptly. 
7.4.2 Non-providing of transaction log and d ata Modification Log in the 
database 
Guidelines for Indian Government Websites and Applications ( GIGW) guidelines 
stipulate that for secure database, audit trail logs should be enabled i.e. every transaction 
should be properly trailed and shown to the intended user whenever required . The 
transaction should always accompany its creator and modifier/updater on user interface. 
Facilitating such event through database helps the system to auto identify the user who 
entered the particular transaction and the modifier, if it has been modified at any stage 
during the process.  
Audit examination of the structures of Database tables used in BEAMS, JKPaySys and 
TreasuryNet applications revealed that though the audit trail table was available in each 
of these applications, the system does not record the complete trail of the transactions 
such as who inserted the records and who modified them.  
The Grey box security audit 21 conducted on BEAMS in the year 2018  also raised this 
issue but the issue was shown resolved just by providing the Login and logout trail. 
Thus, by not providing the transactional logs or data modification logs on the user 
interface, the system would encourage the unauthorised insertion, deletion or updation 
in the database without any accountability.  
In reply, DGAT/ Department  accepted (June 2024 / November 2024 ) the audit 
observation and assured that corrective action would be taken in next version of IFMS. 
 
 
21  Security audit conducted by empaneled Agencies as per the Guidelines of MeitY. 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
118 
 
7.4.3  Formulation of Disaster Recovery and Business Continuity plan 
A Disaster Recovery (DR) plan focuses on bringing the system back online ensuring 
data protection and preventing damages to the system while a Business Continuity plan 
(BCP) aims at developing a proactive process that would keep the system alive and 
operating in case of a major crisis.  
Mission Mode Project ( MMP) guidelines lay emphasis on “Disaster Recovery and 
Business Continuity plan” to ensure that the system runs even in the case of long power 
outages, floods, earthquake, virus attacks and situations like vendor abandoning the 
project etc.  
As per DPR – Security Feature, IFMS Data and application will be kept at the NIC/ State 
Data Centre. Provision for secure network access to the data and application would be 
through various firewall and other security tools. Provision for disaster recovery 
infrastructure, regular backup scheduling and data would be kept at multiple locations. 
For this , funds amounting to ` 97.50 lakh w ere proposed in the DPR for BCP and 
Disaster Recovery Site.  Audit observed that as of March 2024, the IFMS system was  
running at NIC Mini Data Centre without proposed State Data Centre for DR and BCP 
leaving the system exposed to the data loss risks. 
While accepting the audit observation , DGAT/ Department  stated (June 2024 / 
November 2024) that the applications are running in the NIC Mini Data Centre, Jammu 
where backup of the data is regularly maintained, and one copy of the data is safely 
retained at the Civil Secretariat. It was further stated that the  plan for shifting the 
application to SDC or National Cloud with facility of DR was under consideration. 
The reply is not acceptable as the backups taken by the NIC were kept in the same 
premises as NIC Mini Data Centre, in which the application was hosted. Non-sensitivity 
towards such critical aspect may result in the implementing agency not having any  
fall-back point to restore the system in the event of a disaster or any cyber threat. 
7.4.4  Over-reliance on NIC-J&K for System Administration 
As per the MoU entered  (May 2012) between Finance department, GoJ&K, NIC and 
NICSI- New Delhi , maintenance of IFMS applications viz. (BEAMS, JKPayS ys, 
TreasuryNet and GRAS) and administrative functions were to be controlled by Director 
General Accounts and Treasuries (DGAT), Finance Department, GoJK, and NIC-J&K 
was designated as system integrator/developer.  NIC was also responsible to  
provide hosting space in its Data Centre at Jammu on mutually agreeable terms until 
the State Data Centre (SDC) became operational. 
A Project Management Unit (PMU) was established (Feb ruary 2012) to oversee the 
monitoring the Application deployment, Hardware installation, Networking, Data 
centre setup, support and maintenance throughout the project period. As per MoU, the 
manpower for PMU shall be decided through tender amongst NICSI empanelled 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
119 
 
agencies under the aegis of NIC -JK and Information Technology Department of the 
State Government.  
The SDC was made operational in June 2022 by the IT Department, J&K, which is the 
nodal department for implementation of IT related projects in the State/ UT. However, 
as per DGAT, the IT Department, J&K  did not take  up the IFMS project for 
computerisation of treasuries, and migration of applications to SDC for better utilisation 
of cloud-based infrastructure.   
Consequently, a  new Project Monitoring Unit (PMU) in Finance Department was 
created (May 2023) for maintenance, up -gradation and modifications of IT enabled 
systems being implemented in the Finance Department22.  
Audit observed that only BEAMS application was shifted to new Project Monitoring 
Unit (PMU) in Finance Department from the earlier PMU under the aegis of NIC-JK. 
However, o ther IFMS applications 23 are still being managed  by PMU NIC -JK and 
continued to be hosted on NIC mini –Data Centre which is not a dedicated data centre 
for IFMS application. Consequently, maintenance, up-gradation and modifications for 
these applications remained with NIC-JK. 
With respect to the administration control of the system, NIC-JK did not provide any 
user interface for the Finance Department to perform the administrative functions such 
as onboarding/ deboarding of users of the IFMS application, resetting of user’s login, 
inserting/updating/deleting master data of IFMS applications etc.  
Therefore, the continuous reliance and exposure of sensitive data to system integrator/ 
developer/ support team (PMU -NICJK) poses threat to the security of system and the 
applications remain to be hosted on the NIC Mini data centre  even after the lapse of  
12 years. Additionally, transactional or data modification logs are not being maintained.  
DGAT admitted (March 2025) that onboarding/  deboarding of users of BEAMS was 
done by Budget controlling authorities while in respect of other application the same 
was done by NIC on request from the Department. 
Thus, the key administrative functions of IFMS application had not been taken over by 
the GoJ&K. 
 
 
 
 
 
22  BEAMS, JKPaysys, PROOF, EMPOWERMENT portal, E -Audit portal, Mera Vetan, GRAS, GPF . system, 
DBT, PFMS and E-Billing System etc. 
23  JKPaySys, GRAS, E-Audit and TreasuryNet. 

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
120 
 
7.5  Monitoring and Evaluation 
7.5.1  Monitoring and evaluation of IFMS applications 
7.5.1.1 Non conduct of testing of applications 
As per DPR, a Core Consultants Group (CCG) shall be formed by the user Department, 
comprising Subject Experts who shall ensure that the user interface fulfils the usability 
requirements.  
Audit observed that no User Acceptance Testing (UAT) had been conducted except in 
case of TreasuryNet in 2011 as of March 2024 . In absence of testing, it could not be 
ensured whether the software application meets the user requirements and is free of 
defects. Frequent additions of modules were indicative that the system was made live 
without testing.  
Without UAT, there is a higher risk that the application may not fully meet the needs 
or expectations of the users. This misalignment can lead to dissatisfaction and reduced 
productivity. Further, UAT helps identify bugs and issues that might not be uncovered 
during earlier testing phases. Without it, users might encounter critical errors  
post-deployment, impacting the software’s functionality and reliability as discussed in 
Paragraph 7.3.4.1 (III). 
In reply,  DGAT/ Department stated  (June 2024 / November 2024 ) that the 
implementation of applications was done in a phased manner after successful and 
satisfactory response of the pilot project implemented in five Treasuries. It was further 
stated that after thorough testing by the PMU and fixing of bugs as reported by the end 
users, the PMU constituted (December 2011) a committee for implementation of the 
project and all modifications were carried out on the recommendations of the 
committee.  
The reply is not acceptable as documents related to User Acceptance Testing were not 
produced to audit except in case of TreasuryNet application. Further, since no UAT was 
conducted, the effectiveness and user satisfaction of various modules and applications 
developed in IFMS could not be assessed. 
During Exit Conference Department accepted the audit observation and (October 2025) 
assured that future enhancements will follow formal UAT documentation and business 
process mapping before deployment. 
7.5.1.2 Non-Certification by Standardisation Testing and Quality Certification 
Paragraph 1.6.3 of DPR provides the need of certification and testing of  IFMS 
applications by an independent agency like Standardisation Testing and Quality 
Certification (STQC) to ensure quality standards. 
For conformity of the product to the requirement and the demonstrability of its quality, 
following management certification scheme/ certification were to be provided by the 
STQC of IT related projects: 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
121 
 
• ISO 9001 Quality Management System (QSM) Certification 
• ISO 14001 Environment Management System (EMS) Certification 
• ISO 27001 Information Security Management System (ISMS) Certification 
• ISO 20000 IT Service Management (ISMS) Certification 
Further, the several website in J&K (May 2025) were taken down by the GoJK 
including JKPaySys  due to non -compliance to the cyber security protocols and 
vulnerability assessment and penetration testing guidelines. 
Audit observed the following in this regard: 
• Neither the implementing agency i.e. NIC-JK nor the GoJ&K put any efforts towards 
making the IFMS tested against industry and safety standards  and for certification 
as per provision of DPR.  
• The process of identifying , classifying and valuing the assets  and estimating 
expected loss due to risks was not undertaken.  
• The risk register had also not been prepared by the Project Management as required 
under ISO 20000 IT Service Management (ISMS) Certification. 
As per IT Act 2008 , it is mandatory to have security audit of all the  
application and web services to be eligible for hosting in State Data Centre (SDC). 
Failure to obtain these certificates forces the department to host IFMS on NIC Mini 
Data Centre. Also unofficial email IDs are being used by the users like G-mail, dummy 
e-mails etc. thereby increasing the risks. 
In reply, Department stated (November 2024) that matter was taken up with NIC who 
assured that necessary step would be taken in next version of IFMS. 
7.5.2  Conclusion 
The Business Process Re-engineering process was deficient and GoJ&K had not formed 
Core Consultant Group as of March 2023 for implementation of IFMS in consideration 
of user requirements. Due to non -revision of DPR as per GoI’s instructions and  
non-achievement of prescribed milestones of MMP -Treasury computerisation 
guidelines, the State/ UT had lost the central assistance of ` 11.88 crore for 
implementation of IFMS.  
Instead of carrying out upgradation of SDC for hosting/storage of IFMS applications, 
NIC irregularly hosted all the applications of IFMS on NIC Mini Data Centre at Jammu 
without any supporting backup site while the funding for the same remained 
mis utilised. 
The implementation of IFMS in J&K is characterised by various deficiencies and 
significant delays. As of March 2023, only four out of the twelve proposed modules 
were fully operational, leading to delay of more than a decade in full implementation 
of IFMS. In BEAMS and JKPaySys modules, there were serious shortcomings in data 
validation, which further compromise system integrity and usability. The lack of  

Composite Audit Report (Civil and PSEs) for the period ended March 2023  
 
122 
 
client-side and server-side validation has led to submission of erroneous and incomplete 
data, negatively impacting budget allocation and overall data management. Lack of 
integration among key financial applications namely BEAMS and TreasuryNet reflect 
the technical inefficiency of the system in ensuring seamless data transfer. This not only 
resulted in excess expenditure over allocation but also rendered the system susceptible 
to unauthorised modification in bill particulars like amount of bill, DDO code, Major 
Head, Detailed Head, Scheme code and Treasury voucher Numbers.  
There was absence of robust logical access controls, effective password recovery 
options and transaction log in which creates opportunities for unauthorised access and 
manipulation of sensitive information. System does not provide for complete trail of 
the transactions such as who inserted the records and who modified them.  Lack of a 
comprehensive Disaster Recovery and Business Continuity Plan exposes the system to 
risks of data integrity and availability in case of unforeseen events like cyber -attacks 
and operational disruptions. Administrative control of IFMS was not taken over by the 
DGAT even after the lapse of over 12 years resulted in continuous exposure of sensitive 
data. 
The a bsence of User Acceptance Testing (UAT) raise s concerns about software  
fulfilling user requirements and functioning effectively. The failure of the department 
to secure certifications from the Standardisation Testing and Quality Certification 
(STQC) agency indicates that the project did not adhere to industry standards for quality 
assurance, risk assessment, and management certification. 
7.5.3  Recommendations 
In light of the audit findings, GoJ&K may consider: 
• Establishing Core Consultant Group of experts and consultants for proper 
guidance, monitoring, and decision -making during the project’s 
implementation. 
• Conducting a comprehensive Business Process Re -engineering and 
Requirements Analysis to align the IFMS system with the modern business 
rules and practices. 
• Establishing a clear timeline and task force for early completion of the 
remaining eight modules to ensure full system functionality.  
• Implementing both client-side and server-side validation across all modules 
of IFMS.  
• Ensuring strict compliance with established guidelines for user 
authentication, data submission and financial transactions.  
• Ensure timely integration among IFMS applications for data integrity. 
• Securing access controls by implementation of role -based access control 
and Multi-Factor Authentication. 

Chapter-VII: IT Audit of Integrated Financial Management System in J&K 
123 
• Strengthening of Password Management policy by implementing auto
password expiry and password recovery options.
• Audit trail of all transactions or data modifications be maintained.
• Developing a Disaster Recovery Plan by introducing backup strategies,
recovery time objectives and recovery point objectives.
• Ensuring that UAT processes are thoroughly documented, capturing all
test cases, results, issues identified and their resolutions.
• Engaging end-users and stakeholders during the design and development
stages of remaining eight modules  to ensure their requirements are well
understood and reflected in the system.
• Ensuring certification from the Standardisation Testing and Quality
Certification (STQC) agency to demonstrate adherence to industry-standard
practices.
